A Formal Power Series Approach to Multiplicative Dynamic Feedback Interconnection
Kurusch Ebrahimi-Fard, Venkatesh G. S

Abstract
. The goal of the paper is multi-fold. First, an explicit formula is derived to compute the non-commutative generating series of a closed-loop system when a (multi-input, multi-output) plant, given in Chen–Fliess series description is in multiplicative output feedback interconnection with another system, also given as Chen–Fliess series. Furthermore, it is shown that the multiplicative dynamic output feedback connection has a natural interpretation as a transformation group acting on the plant. A computational framework for computing the generating series for multiplicative dynamic output feedback is devised utilizing the Hopf algebras of the coordinate functions corresponding to the shuffle group and the multiplicative feedback group. The pre–Lie algebra in multiplicative feedback is shown to be an example of Foissy’s com-pre-Lie algebras indexed by matrices with certain structure. Key words : Chen–Fliess series; feedback; Hopf algebras; pre- and post-Lie algebras; com-pre-Lie algebra. MSC2020 : 93C10 (primary), 16T05 (primary), 16T30 (secondary), 17D25 (secondary)



1.
Introduction

The document studies multiplicative feedback interconnections of input-output systems using the framework of Chen–Fliess functional series
[
11
]
. As such, there is no need for a state space realization and thus, the results presented here are independent of any state space embedding when a realization is possible
[
12
]
. It was shown in
[
20
]
that
additive feedback
interconnection of two such systems results in a Chen–Fliess series description for the closed–loop system. An efficient computation of the generating series for such closed–loop systems is facilitated through use of tools from combinatorial Hopf algebra
[
7
,
17
]
.

Multiplicative feedback
is another vital interconnection of dynamical systems that is extensively studied in control theory and communications
[
24
,
25
]
. However, when the nature of feedback interconnection becomes
multiplicative
, a description in terms of combinatorial Hopf algebra has been unexplored. It is known that, in the single-input single-output (SISO) setting, the closed-loop system in the affine feedback case (of which multiplicative feedback is a special case) has a Chen–Fliess series description and the computation of feedback formula is facilitated through combinatorial Hopf algebra of coordinate functions
[
19
]
(see also
[
15
]
).

The contribution of the article at hand are two–fold, combining
systems theory
and
algebra
. Regarding the former,

(i)

It is shown that in the multi-input multi-output (MIMO) setting the closed-loop system under multiplicative feedback has a Chen–Fliess series representation. An explicit expression of the closed-loop generating series is provided, which will be called
multiplicative dynamic feedback product
.

(ii)

The invariance of class and relative degree of a dynamical system (plant) under the multiplicative feedback connection is shown. The invariance of relative degree of the plant is essential in the design of feedback linearization of the closed–loop system, a question which is deferred for the future work.

(iii)

It will be shown that the closed–loop multiplicative feedback product has a natural interpretation as a transformation group acting on the plant. The transformation group, denoted by
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)
, is the shuffle group on vectors with formal power series over non-commuting set of indeterminates
X
, whose constant term is
1
in each coordinate, as entries.

(iv)

Prior to discussing transformation group, it is essential to describe the unit group
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star\right)
, that arises from cascade interconnection of Chen–Fliess series along with their multiplicative feedforward of inputs. It turns out to be the Grossman–Larson group of the pre-group
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,},\curvearrowleft\right)
. The details of action map
\curvearrowleft
are to be found in Section
2.4
. For more details on pre-groups in general, we refer to
[
1
,
3
]
.

Part of the work described so far has appeared in earlier work of the first author
[
38
]
.

The contributions towards the algebra side are briefly tip-toed as follows:

(i)

The algorithmic framework for the computation of the multiplicative dynamic feedback product formula for a general MIMO case is devised using a connected graded bialgebra
\mathcal{H}
, which is shown to be right-handed (in the sense of
[
29
]
).

(ii)

The character group of the Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}
is isomorphic to a normal subgroup of
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star\right)
(details in Section
5
).

(iii)

A cointeracting Hopf algebra structure
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\Delta,\epsilon\right)
is shown to result from dualizing the pre-group structure mentioned earlier. The coproducts
\Delta
dualizes dynamic feedback group product denoted
\star
. The action map
\curvearrowleft
dualizes to the coaction map
\rho
.

(iv)

Linearizing the right-handed coproduct
\Delta
results in a graded right pre-Lie coalgebra and its graded dual defines a graded right pre-Lie product on the space of non-commutative polynomials with constant term being zero in each coordinate, denoted by
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle
. Linearizing the coaction map and taking its graded dual gives a right pre-Lie product. In fact, it is shown that
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$},\triangleleft\right)
is a special case of Foissy’s
com-pre-Lie
algebras.

(v)

We show that over a field of characteristic zero, every com-pre-Lie algebra inherits a new pre-Lie product (Theorem
7.1
). The two pre-Lie products anti-symmetrize to same Lie bracket; the multiplicative feedback case is a prime example of this observation.

The paper is organized as follows. The next section provides a brief summary of Chen–Fliess series and their interconnections. Section
2.4
builds the pivotal
multiplicative dynamic output feedback group
that acts as a transformation group for the multiplicative feedback interconnection. Section
3
is where the closed–loop configuration of Chen–Fliess series is analyzed. The invariance of relative degree under multiplicative output feedback is asserted in Section
4
. A framework for computing the feedback product is devised in Section
5
including examples. The discussion on pre-Lie products and com-pre-Lie algebras are carried out in Sections
6
&
7

1.1.
Preliminaries: Formal Power Series

We set notation by recalling some preliminaries about non-commutative formal power series and various algebraic structures, including bialgebras. For more details regarding the latter, we refer to
[
2
,
6
,
28
,
31
,
32
]
.

Let
X=\{x_{0},x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\}
denote a finite alphabet of non-commuting indeterminates.

(1)

{\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle
is the
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebra of non-commutative polynomials with indeterminates in
X
. As a vector space, it is spanned by the set of words, denoted by
X^{\ast}
, with letters from
X
. The unit is the empty word, which we write
\mathbf{1}
, and the set of non-empty words is denoted by
X^{+}:=X^{*}\backslash\{\mathbf{1}\}
.

(2)

{\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle
is a cocommutative Hopf algebra, graded (by the word length,
|w|\geq 0
,
w\in X^{*}
) and connected, with catenation product and counit
\epsilon
such that
\epsilon(w)=\delta_{|w|,0}
for any
w\in X^{*}
. The coproduct is unshuffle
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}
for which the elements
x_{i}\in X
are primitive, i.e.,
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}(x_{i})=x_{i}\otimes\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{1}\otimes x_{i}
. A few examples of this coproduct are given next

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}x_{i}x_{j}

\displaystyle=x_{i}x_{j}\otimes\mathbf{1}+x_{i}\otimes x_{j}+x_{j}\otimes x_{i}+\mathbf{1}\otimes x_{i}x_{j},

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}x_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=x_{i}^{2}\otimes\mathbf{1}+2x_{i}\otimes x_{i}+\mathbf{1}\otimes x_{i}^{2}.

In general, with
x_{i}^{0}:=\mathbf{1}
, we have

(1)

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}x_{i}^{n}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}x_{i}^{k}\otimes x_{i}^{n-k}.

(3)

Remark
:
Traditionally, the counit of
{\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle
is denoted by
\emptyset
in the Chen–Fliess literature; for the rest of the article the presentation also follows this custom.

(4)

The convolution algebra of linear maps from the counital coalgebra
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle$},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\right)
to
{\mathbb{R}}
, is given by the space of formal power series denoted by
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
. The dual basis is given by
\{\emptyset\}\cup X^{+}
, such that
\eta\left(\xi\right)=1
if
\eta=\xi
in
X^{+}
, and zero else. An element
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is represented by

\displaystyle c=c(\mathbf{1})\emptyset+\sum\limits_{\eta\in X^{+}}c(\eta)\eta.

In the following,
\emptyset
is not explicitly written unless needed.

The convolution product on
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
is the shuffle product, which is defined for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
p\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle$}
by

\displaystyle\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)(p)

\displaystyle=m_{{\mathbb{R}}}\circ\left(c\otimes d\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}(p).

Here,
m_{{\mathbb{R}}}
is the usual product in
{\mathbb{R}}
and the unit element is the counit
\emptyset
.

(5)

The shuffle group
\left(M,{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)
is the normal subgroup of the unit group of
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
where

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\{1\emptyset+c^{\prime}:c^{\prime}\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},c^{\prime}(\mathbf{1})=0\}.

(6)

Consider arbitrary
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
p,q\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle$}
. The (left) shift operation
p^{-1}
on the shuffle algebra
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)
is defined by
p^{-1}(c)(q):=c(pq)
. For primitive elements
x_{i}\in X\subsetneq\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle$}
, the left shift is a derivation on the shuffle algebra viz.

\displaystyle x_{i}^{-1}\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(x_{i}^{-1}\left(c\right)\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d+c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(x_{i}^{-1}\left(d\right)\right).

Looking ahead, we define for
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
the Chen–Fliess series
F_{c}[u](t)
as a functional series such that the basis (words) of
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
are identified with iterated integrals of the function
u
, a control signal (detailed in Section
2
). In the context of a dynamical system, a Chen–Fliess series expresses its input-output behavior and provides a coordinate-independent framework to analyze the intrinsic properties of the system.

(1)

{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
can also be considered a non-commutative
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebra under catenation product, defined for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
by

\displaystyle c.d\left(\eta\right)=\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta,\mu\in X^{*}\\
\zeta\mu=\eta\end{subarray}}c\left(\zeta\right)d\left(\mu\right).

(2)

An element
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}:=\{c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}:c(\mathbf{1})=0\}
is termed
proper
series. Whereas
\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{np}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}:=\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\setminus\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
contains non-proper series. An element
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
which has
finite support
, is termed
proper
polynomial. The set of proper polynomials are denoted by
{\mathbb{R}}_{p}\langle X\rangle
.

(3)

{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
is a complete topological vector space endowed by ultrametric
\kappa
defined as
\kappa(c,d)=\sigma^{-val\left(c-d\right)},
for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
\sigma\in]0,1[
. Here
val\left(\cdot\right)
is the valuation of a series. See
[
4
]
for details.

(4)

Consider the
\ell
-fold Cartesian product
\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\;\cong\;\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\cdots\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
as complete topological vector space. Thus, a Chen–Fliess series
F_{c}
for
c=\left(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{\ell}\right)\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is a vector of Chen–Fliess series
\left(F_{c_{1}},F_{c_{2}},\ldots,F_{c_{\ell}}\right)
.

(5)

\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)
is a direct product of shuffle algebras,
\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)_{j}=c_{j}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{j}
,
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
.

(6)

Following Foissy
[
15
]
,
{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
can also inherit associative (but non-commutative) algebra structure via “adorned” shuffle products, denoted
{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{k}
, where the subscript
k=1,2,\ldots,\ell
. For
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}

\displaystyle c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{k}d=\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{k}\\
c_{2}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{k}\\
\vdots\\
c_{\ell}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{k}\end{pmatrix}.

(7)

In general, for a given
\mathsf{a}=\left(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{\ell}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
; define the weighted product
c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{\mathsf{a}}d=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i}\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\right)
, then
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{\mathsf{a}}\right)
is an associative (but non-commutative) algebra
[
15
]
. It follows from the next identity

\displaystyle c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}\left(d{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{j}e\right)=\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{j}e=\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{j}e\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\qquad c,d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}.

(8)

The products
{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}
and
{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}
are
mixed
associative, a property that is particularly made use of in Section
7
viz for all
c,d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
:

\displaystyle\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}e=\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}e\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\quad\text{ and }\quad\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}d\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}e=c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{i}\left(d{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}e\right).

(9)

Consider
\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}^{\ell}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\;\cong\;\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\cdots\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
as topological vector subspace. The following set notation is thus imminent:

{\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{\ell}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{np}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{np}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\times\cdots\times\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{np}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}

and
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{\ell}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is called
purely improper
. As
\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{np}^{\ell}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}=\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\setminus\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}^{\ell}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, purely improper is thus a stronger notion than just non–proper when
\ell>1
.

(10)

A series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
can be decomposed as

c=c_{N}+c_{F},

where
c_{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\langle\langle X_{0}\rangle\rangle
,
X_{0}:=\{x_{0}\}
, is the so-called
natural part
of
c
and
c_{F}:=c-c_{N}
is termed its
forced part
.

(11)

{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}
is treated as a commutative algebra under pointwise product with unit element
{\rm\,l\!l}=(1,1,\ldots,1)
.

2.
Chen–Fliess Series and the Interconnections

2.1.
Chen–Fliess Series

Let
\mathfrak{p}\geq 1
and
T>0
be given. For a Lebesgue measurable function
u:[0,T]\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
, define

\|u\|_{\mathfrak{p}}=\max\{\|u_{i}\|_{\mathfrak{p}}:\ 1\leq i\leq m\}
, where
\|u_{i}\|_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the usual

L_{\mathfrak{p}}
-norm for a measurable real-valued function,
u_{i}
, defined on
[0,T]
. Let
L^{m}_{\mathfrak{p}}[0,T]

denote the set of all measurable functions defined on
[0,T]
having a finite
\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak{p}}
norm and

B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}(R)[0,T]:=\{u\in L_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}[0,T]:\|u\|_{\mathfrak{p}}\leq R\}
.

Given any series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, the corresponding

Chen–Fliess series
is

(2)

F_{c}[u](t)=\sum_{\eta\in X^{\ast}}c(\eta)\,F_{\eta}[u](t),

where
F_{\emptyset}[u]:=1
and

F_{x_{i}\bar{\eta}}[u](t):=\int_{0}^{t}u_{i}(\tau)F_{\bar{\eta}}[u](\tau)\,d\tau

with
x_{i}\in X
,
\bar{\eta}\in X^{+}\cup\{\emptyset\}
, and
u_{0}:=1

[
11
]
. If there exist constants
K,M>0
such that

(3)

\displaystyle\lvert c_{i}(\eta)\rvert\leq KM^{\lvert\eta\rvert}\lvert\eta\rvert!,\;\;

for all
\eta\in X^{\ast}
and
i=1,\ldots,\ell
, then
F_{c}
constitutes a well-defined mapping from
B_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m}(R)[0,T]
into
B_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\ell}(S)[0,\,T]
for sufficiently small
R,T>0
, where the numbers
\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}\in[1,\infty]
are conjugate exponents, i.e.,
1/\mathfrak{p}+1/\mathfrak{q}=1

[
22
]
. A series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
with coefficients obeying the growth condition in (
3
) is called a
locally convergent
generating series. The set of all locally convergent generating series is denoted by
{\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}_{LC}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
. The details regarding convergence of Chen–Fliess series with respect to the growth of their generating series are well-detailed in the literature
[
34
,
39
,
40
]
. The expansion (
2
) is called a Fliess operator if
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}_{LC}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
otherwise it defines an operator only in a formal sense.

Remark
:  The shuffle product on
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
corresponds to encoding the
integration by parts
rule of the Lebesgue–Stieltjes integration
[
30
]
.

2.2.
Interconnections of Chen–Fliess Series

Given two Chen–Fliess series,
F_{c}
and
F_{d}
,
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, the parallel and product interconnections
[
11
,
30
]
,
F_{c}+F_{d}=F_{c+d}
and
F_{c}F_{d}=F_{c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d}
respectively, both preserve local convergence
[
33
,
34
]
. For
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{k}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, where
X^{\prime}=\{x^{\prime}_{0},x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{\ell}\}
, the composition, or cascade interconnection,
F_{c}\circ F_{d}
, has a Chen–Fliess series representation
F_{c\circ d}\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{k}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, defined via the
composition product

[
9
,
10
]
where

(4)

c\circ d=\sum_{\eta\in X^{\prime\ast}}c(\eta)\,\psi_{d}(\eta)(\emptyset).

The continuous (in ultrametric topology) algebra homomorphism
\psi_{d}:\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle X^{\prime}\rangle$}\rightarrow{\rm End}\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\right)
maps
x_{i}^{\prime}\eta\longmapsto\psi_{d}(x_{i}^{\prime}\eta)
and is defined for all
i=0,1,\ldots,\ell
and any
e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
by

\psi_{d}(x_{i}^{\prime})(e)=x_{0}(d_{i}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}e),\qquad d_{0}:=\emptyset.

By definition,
\psi_{d}(\emptyset)
is the identity map on
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
. The cascade interconnection preserves local convergence
[
33
]
and the composition product (
4
) distributes over the shuffle product.

Theorem 2.1
.

[
20
]

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{k}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
and
e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, such that
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=\ell+1
, then

(5)

\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)\circ e=\left(c\circ e\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(d\circ e\right).

The composition product preserves the purely improper property of the left argument.

Theorem 2.2
.

If
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{k}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
such that
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=\ell+1
, then
\left(c\circ d\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
. Hence,
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{k}\,\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
if and only if
c\circ d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{k}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and vice-versa. Similarly, if
c
is a proper series then
c\circ d
is a proper series and vice-versa.

Proof:
The proof follows immediately from (
4
).

The composition product (
4
) is a strong contraction map with respect to its right argument in the ultrametric topology.

Theorem 2.3
.

[
20
]

Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{k}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
and
d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{\ell}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, such that
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=\ell+1
, then
\kappa\left(c\circ d,c\circ e\right)\leq\sigma\kappa\left(d,e\right)
where
\sigma\in[0,1[
.

2.3.
Cascading with Multiplicative Feedforward of Input

The cascade interconnection of Chen–Fliess series along with the multiplicative feedforward of the input, as shown in Figure
1
, arises primarily in the analysis of multiplicative feedback interconnection discussed in Section
3
. A semblance of definition of such an interconnection has appeared implicitly (and was limited to the SISO case) in Definition
3.1
of
[
19
]
.

Figure 1
.
Cascade interconnection of Chen–Fliess series
F_{d}
with
F_{c}
along with multiplicative feedforward of input

The input–output map of Figure
1
, the function

y=F_{c}[u.F_{d}[u]]

has Chen–Fliess series representation denoted by
F_{c\curvearrowleft d}
, where the
multiplicative mixed composition product
of
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{p}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is defined by:

(6)

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft d:=\sum_{\eta\in X^{\ast}}c\left(\eta\right)\eta\curvearrowleft d,

where for all
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
the binary operation
\eta\curvearrowleft d
is defined inductively

\displaystyle\emptyset\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle:=\emptyset

\displaystyle x_{0}\eta\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle:=x_{0}\left(\eta\curvearrowleft d\right)

\displaystyle x_{i}\eta\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle:=x_{i}\left(d_{i}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}(\eta\curvearrowleft d)\right)\quad\qquad\forall\,i=1,2,\ldots,m.

The following results were proven in the SISO setting
[
19
]
. Their MIMO extensions are straightforward and thus attributed to the referenced document with the omission of proofs.

The multiplicative mixed composition (
6
) distributes over the shuffle product on the right.

Theorem 2.4
.

[
19
]

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{p}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then

\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)\curvearrowleft e=\left(c\curvearrowleft e\right){\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(d\curvearrowleft e\right).

It is a strong contraction in its right argument in the ultrametric topology.

Theorem 2.5
.

[
19
]

Let
d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{p}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Define
c^{\prime}=c-c(\mathbf{1})
, then

\kappa\left(c\curvearrowleft d,c\curvearrowleft e\right)\leq\sigma^{val\left(c^{\prime}\right)}\kappa\left(d,e\right).

Observe that
val\left(c^{\prime}\right)\geq 1
and
\sigma\in]0,1[
in Theorem
2.5
. The composition product (
4
) and the multiplicative mixed composition product (
6
) are associative in combination.

Theorem 2.6
.

[
19
]

Let
X^{\prime}=\{x^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{p}\}
and
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
. Let
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{p}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then

c\circ\left(d\curvearrowleft e\right)=\left(c\circ d\right)\curvearrowleft e.

2.4.
Multiplicative Dynamic Output Feedback Group

The dynamic multiplicative feedback group plays a vital role in computation of the multiplicative dynamic feedback formula and appeared implicitly (in the simpler SISO setting) in reference
[
19
]
.

Figure 2
.
Cascade interconnection of Chen–Fliess series
F_{d}
with
F_{c}
along with multiplicative feedforward of their inputs.

Figure
2
shows the cascade interconnection of
F_{c}
and
F_{d}
,
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, along with their multiplicative feedforward of inputs. The input-output relation of the composite system is

(7)

\displaystyle y=u.F_{d}[u]F_{c}[u.F_{d}[u]].

Defining the
multiplicative composition product
of
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}

(8)

\displaystyle c\star d:=d{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right),

the right–hand side of (
7
) can be represented by Chen–Fliess series,
y=u.F_{c\star d}[u].

Theorem 2.7
.

[
19
, Lem. 3.6]

Let
c,d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then
c\star{\rm\,l\!l}=c
and

(c\star d)\star e=c\star(d\star e).

We have a right action by the non-commutative monoid
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star,{\rm\,l\!l}\right)
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
.

Theorem 2.8
.

[
19
]

Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then

(9)

\displaystyle\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)\curvearrowleft e=c\curvearrowleft\left(d\star e\right).

The prominent question is to find the invertible elements of the monoid
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star,{\rm\,l\!l}\right)
and the motivation to find the unit elements of the monoid shall be evident in Section
3
. Let
d,e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and suppose

d\star e={\rm\,l\!l}.
Observe that
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
implies
\left(d\curvearrowleft e\right)\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and using Theorem
2.4
yields

\displaystyle e=\left(d\curvearrowleft e\right)^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}=d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft e.

Thus for
e
to be right inverse of
d
, it has to satisfy the fixed point equation

(10)

\displaystyle e=d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft e.

Theorem
2.5
asserts that the map
e\mapsto d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft e
is a strong contraction in the ultrametric space inferring that (
10
) has a unique fixed point.

Suppose
e
is the left inverse of
d
, i.e.,
e\star d={\rm\,l\!l}
, then it satisfies the equation,

(11)

\displaystyle d=e^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft d.

If
e
is a solution of (
10
), then it satisfies (
11
) and vice–versa. Thus,
e
is the unique inverse of
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
denoted
d^{\star-1}
. The case
m=1
of the next theorem is proved in
[
19
]
.

Theorem 2.9
.

(Multiplicative dynamic output feedback group)

\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star,{\rm\,l\!l}\right)
forms a group with the identity element
{\rm\,l\!l}
.

The following statement is a direct interpretation of Theorem
2.8
and the defintion of the group product
\star
.

Theorem 2.10
.

\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,},\curvearrowleft\right)
is a right pre-group with
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\star\right)
as its Grossman–Larson group.

Theorem
2.9
, Theorem
2.4
and (
8
) imply the following relations for all
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
:

(12)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} c^{\star-1}&=c^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft c^{\star-1}\\
\left(c^{\star-1}\right)^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}&=c\curvearrowleft c^{\star-1}.\end{aligned}

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then
\left(c\star d\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)d\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
.

Proof:
Since
\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
, if follows that

\left(c\star d\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=\left(d{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)\left(\mathbf{1}\right)d\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)d\left(\mathbf{1}\right).

Theorem 2.11
.

Let
M^{m}:=\{{\rm\,l\!l}+c\>:c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}^{m}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}\}
, then
\left(M^{m},\star,{\rm\,l\!l}\right)
is a normal subgroup of the multiplicative dynamic feedback group.

Proof:
Follows from Lemma
2.1
.

The group
M^{m}
is isomorphic to the character group of the Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}
which is used for computation of feedback detailed in Section
5
.

3.
Chen–Fliess Series Under Multiplicative Dynamic Output Feedback

Let
F_{c}
be a Chen–Fliess series with
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Suppose it is interconnected with
F_{d}
where
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
, as shown in Figure
3
. Note that,
\lvert X\rvert=m+1
and
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=q+1
.

Figure 3
.
F_{c}
in multiplicative output feedback with
F_{d}

Assume that the closed–loop system has a Chen–Fliess series representation, say
y=F_{e}[v]
, where
e\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Then,

\displaystyle y=F_{e}[v]=F_{c}[u]=F_{c}[vF_{d}[y]]=F_{c}[vF_{d}[F_{e}[v]]]

\displaystyle=F_{c}[vF_{d\circ e}[v]]

\displaystyle=F_{c\curvearrowleft\left(d\circ e\right)}[v],

for any admissible input
v
. Hence, the series
e
is characterized by the fixed point equation

(13)

\displaystyle e=c\curvearrowleft\left(d\circ e\right).

Theorem
2.3
and Theorem
2.5
imply that
e\mapsto c\curvearrowleft\left(d\circ e\right)
yields a strong contraction map in the ultrametric topology. Hence (
13
) has a unique fixed point.

Theorem 3.1
.

The series
c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is the unique fixed point of the map
e\mapsto c\curvearrowleft\left(d\circ e\right)
in the ultrametric space
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$},\kappa\right)
.

Proof:
Let
e=c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}
, then using Theorem
2.6
and Theorem
2.4
, gives

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft\left(d\circ e\right)

\displaystyle=c\curvearrowleft\left[d\circ\left(c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=c\curvearrowleft\left[\left(d\circ c\right)\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\right]

\displaystyle=c\curvearrowleft\left[\left(d\circ c\right)^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\right]^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq:distrib})}}{{=}}c\curvearrowleft\left[\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\right]^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eqn:inverse_relations})}}{{=}}c\curvearrowleft\left[\left(\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule
