\displaystyle=c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}=e.

Theorem 3.2
.

Given series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$}
(such that
\lvert X\rvert=m+1
and
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=q+1
), the generating series for the closed–loop system in Figure
3
is given by the multiplicative dynamic feedback product

(14)

\displaystyle c\check{@}d:=c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}.

The notion that feedback can be described in mathematical terms as a transformation group acting on the plant is well established in control theory
[
5
]
. The following theorem describes the situation in the present context.

Theorem 3.3
.

The multiplicative dynamic feedback product (
14
) is a right group action by the group
\left(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,},{\rm\,l\!l}\right)
on the set
{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
, where
\lvert X\rvert=m+1
and
\lvert X^{\prime}\rvert=q+1
.

Proof:
Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{q}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Theorem
3.2
gives

\displaystyle c\check{@}{\rm\,l\!l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{multidynfeedbprod})}}{{=}}c\curvearrowleft\left({\rm\,l\!l}^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}=c\curvearrowleft{\rm\,l\!l}=c.

\displaystyle=\left(c\curvearrowleft\left(d_{1}^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule Therefore, the statement is confirmed as the following identity holds

\displaystyle\left(c\check{@}d_{1}\right)\check{@}d_{2}

\displaystyle=c\check{@}\left(d_{1}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{2}\right).

Remark
:  The transformation group for the
additive dynamic feedback product
is the additive group
(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$},+,0)
while for multiplicative feedback, it is
(\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}^{m}\,\langle\langle X^{\prime}\rangle\rangle$},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,},{\rm\,l\!l})
.

4.
Invariance of Class and Relative Degree

The notion of relative degree of a plant is essential in studying feedback linearization and system inversion
[
25
]
as well as flatness
[
26
]
. The definitions of class and relative degree of a SISO Chen–Fliess series are characterized by the
structure
of its generating series
[
18
,
21
,
34
]
. The definition of relative degree of a Chen–Fliess series is consistent with the classical definition whenever
y=F_{c}[u]
has an input-affine analytic state space realization
[
18
,
19
]
.

In this section, the alphabet
X=\{x_{0},x_{1}\}
and
{\rm supp}(c)
denotes the support of
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Recall that
c_{N},c_{F}
are the natural respectively forced part of the series
c
. Recall that we write
1
for
1\emptyset
in
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
.

4.1.
Class

Definition 4.1
.

[
21
]

A series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
is said to be of
r
–class, denoted by
\mathscr{C}(c)=r
, if
{\rm supp}(c_{F})\subseteq x_{0}^{r-1}X^{+}
and
{\rm supp}(c_{F})\nsubseteq x_{0}^{r}X^{+}
. By definition,
\mathscr{C}(c)=\infty
if
c_{F}=0
.

Lemma 4.1
.

[
21
]

Every series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
has a class.

Example 4.1
.

Let
c=1+x_{0}x_{1}^{2}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}
. Thus,
c_{F}=x_{0}x_{1}^{2}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}
, which implies
{\rm supp}(c_{F})\subseteq x_{0}X^{+}
but
{\rm supp}(c_{F})\nsubseteq x_{0}^{2}X^{+}
. Thus,
\mathscr{C}(c)=2
.

The following lemma is essential in quantifying the class of
c\curvearrowleft d
for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
c,c^{\prime},d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
such that
{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
. Then

i)\;x_{0}^{k}\curvearrowleft d=x_{0}^{k}\;\;\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}\qquad ii)\;c_{N}\curvearrowleft d=c_{N}\qquad iii)\;{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}.

Proof:
Statements
i)
and
ii)
are clear from (
6
).

Statement
iii)
follows from
{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
implying the existence of
x_{i}\eta\in{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)
where
x_{i}\neq x_{0}
and
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
. Then,
x_{i}\eta\curvearrowleft d=x_{i}\left(d_{i}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(\eta\curvearrowleft d\right)\right)
. Therefore,
{\rm supp}\left(x_{i}\eta\curvearrowleft d\right)\subseteq x_{i}X^{\ast}
implies that
{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}X^{\ast}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then the
r
-class
\mathscr{C}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)=\mathscr{C}\left(c\right)
.

Proof:
Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
be of
r
–class
\mathscr{C}\left(c\right)
, then
c
can be decomposed as
c=c_{N}+x_{0}^{r-1}c^{\prime}
,
where
c^{\prime}
is a proper series such that
{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}X^{\ast}
. Then,

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle=\left(c_{N}\curvearrowleft d\right)+x_{0}^{r-1}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)=c_{N}+x_{0}^{r-1}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right),

with
{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)\not\subseteq x_{0}\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
. Hence,
{\rm supp}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{F}\subseteq x_{0}^{r-1}X^{+}
and
{\rm supp}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{F}\not\subseteq x_{0}^{r}X^{+}
.

Example 4.2
.

Consider
c
in Example
4.1
and
d=1+x_{1}\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Then
\mathscr{C}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)=2=\mathscr{C}\left(c\right)
as
c\curvearrowleft d=1+x_{0}x_{1}^{2}+3x_{0}x_{1}^{3}+3x_{0}x_{1}^{4}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}^{2}.

The multiplicative dynamic feedback product preserves the class of its left argument.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
be of
r
–class and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then
\mathscr{C}\left(c\check{@}d\right)=r=\mathscr{C}\left(c\right)
.

Proof:
Observe that
\mathscr{C}(c\check{@}d)=\mathscr{C}(c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1})=r=\mathscr{C}\left(c\right).

Example 4.3
.

Consider
c=x_{1}
and
d=1+\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}k!x_{1}^{k}
in
{\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
. Theorem
3.2
implies
\mathscr{C}\left(c\check{@}d\right)=\mathscr{C}\left(c\right)=1
as
c\check{@}d=x_{1}+x_{1}x_{0}x_{1}+3x_{1}x_{0}x_{1}x_{0}x_{1}+4x_{1}x_{0}^{2}x_{1}^{2}+\cdots.

4.2.
Relative Degree

Definition 4.2
.

[
21
]

A series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
has relative degree
r
if
\mathscr{C}(c)=r
and the word
x_{0}^{r-1}x_{1}\in{\rm supp}(c_{F})
. Otherwise,
c
does not have relative degree.

The following theorem quantifies the relative degree of
c\curvearrowleft d
for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
.

Theorem 4.3
.

If
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
with relative degree
r_{c}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
such that
d\left(\mathbf{1}\right)\neq 0
, then
c\curvearrowleft d
has relative degree
r_{c}
.

Proof:
From Theorem
4.1
,
\mathscr{C}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)=r_{c}
. It remains to prove that
x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}\in{\rm supp}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)
. Since
c
has relative degree
r_{c}
, it can be decomposed as:
c=c_{N}+\lambda x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}+x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}c^{\prime}
, where
\lambda\neq 0
and
c^{\prime}
is a proper series such that
x_{1}\not\in{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)
. Then,

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle=\left(c_{N}\curvearrowleft d\right)+\lambda\left(x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}\curvearrowleft d\right)+\left(x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right).

Using (
6
) and Lemma
4.2
,
c\curvearrowleft d=c_{N}+\lambda x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}d+x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)
. Since
d=\alpha+d^{\prime}
, where
\alpha=d\left(\mathbf{1}\right)\neq 0
and
d^{\prime}\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=0
. Hence,

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft d=c_{N}+\lambda\alpha x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}+x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}x_{1}d^{\prime}+x_{0}^{r_{c}-1}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)\quad\left(\alpha\lambda\neq 0\right).

Note that
x_{1}\not\in{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\right)
implies
x_{1}\not\in{\rm supp}\left(c^{\prime}\curvearrowleft d\right)
.

Example 4.4
.

Let
c=1+x_{0}^{2}+x_{0}x_{1}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}
and
d=1+x_{1}
. The relative degree
r_{c}=2
and
d\left(\mathbf{1}\right)=1
. Then
r_{c\curvearrowleft d}=r_{c}
as
c\curvearrowleft d=1+x_{0}^{2}+x_{0}x_{1}+x_{0}x_{1}^{2}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}+x_{0}^{2}x_{1}^{2}.

Theorem 4.4
.

If
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
with relative degree
r_{c}
and
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then
r_{c\check{@}d}=r_{c}
.

Proof:
From Theorem
3.2
, the multiplicative dynamic feedback product

\displaystyle c\check{@}d=c\curvearrowleft\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}.

From
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
being equivalent to
\left(d^{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}-1}\circ c\right)^{\star-1}\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{pi}\,\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
via Theorem
2.2
and Theorem
2.9
, we deduce that
c\check{@}d
has relative degree
r_{c}
by Theorem
4.3
.

Example 4.5
.

Consider Example
4.3
where
r_{c}=1
. Then
r_{c\check{@}d}=1=r_{c}
.

5.
Computational Framework

This subsection presents the Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}
of coordinate functions with character group isomorphic to the normal subgroup
M^{m}
of multiplicative dynamic feedback group.

The vector space
V
of coordinate maps on
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle
is spanned by
a^{j}_{\eta}
, where
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
and
j=1,2,\ldots,m
. For a series
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}

\displaystyle a^{j}_{\eta}\left(c\right)=c_{j}\left(\eta\right),\quad\forall\,j=1,2,\ldots,m.

The vector space
V=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}V_{n}
is graded, where
V_{n}
is spanned by
a^{j}_{\eta}
,
j=1,2,\ldots,m
,
|\eta|=n
.

For all
k=0,1,2,\ldots,m
define a linear endomorphism
\theta_{k}:V\longrightarrow V
such that
\theta_{k}(a^{j}_{\eta})=a^{j}_{x_{k}\eta},
for all
j=1,2,\ldots,m
. Let
\mathcal{B}
denote the graded symmetric algebra with grading induced by
V
. Its product is denoted by
m
and the unit is
\mathsf{1}
.
\mathcal{B}
is a bialgebra with the cocommutative coproduct
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}
defined for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
as

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}(a^{j}_{\eta})\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d\right)_{j}\left(\eta\right)=\left(c_{j}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}d_{j}\right)\left(\eta\right).

By extending the usual unshuffle coproduct on words,
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}(\eta)=\sum_{(\eta)}{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes{\eta^{\prime\prime}}
(employing Sweedler’s notation), it is understood that for all
a^{j}_{\eta}\in V
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)=\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}.

The counit
\nu
is defined as

\displaystyle\nu\left(h\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1;\quad\text{if}\;h=\mathsf{1},a^{1}_{\mathbf{1}},a^{2}_{\mathbf{1}},\ldots,a^{m}_{\mathbf{1}}\,\\
0;\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Theorem 5.1
.

[
13
]

On
V

\displaystyle\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\circ\theta_{k}=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}+{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\theta_{k}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},

for all
k=0,1,2,\ldots,m
.

Remark
:  Observe that
\left(\mathcal{B},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is not a connected graded bialgebra as the elements
a^{j}_{\mathbf{1}}
,
j=1,2,\ldots,m
, are group-like but not invertible.

Denote
\mathfrak{s_{i}}:=\left(a^{i}_{\mathbf{1}}-\mathsf{1}\right)
for
i=1,2,\ldots,m
. The ideal
\left(\mathfrak{s_{1}},\mathfrak{s_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{s_{m}}\right)
, is a bi-ideal. Define
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{B}/\left(\mathfrak{s_{1}},\mathfrak{s_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{s_{m}}\right)
and thus,

Theorem 5.2
.

\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is a graded connected bialgebra. The character group of
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is isomorphic to the shuffle group
\left(M^{m},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)\cong\underbrace{\left(M,{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)\times\left(M,{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)\times\cdots\times\left(M,{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)}_{m\;\text{times}}
.

The reminder of the section is to detail another coalgebra structure compatible with the graded augmented algebra of
\mathcal{H}
.

5.1.
Multiplicative Feedback Bialgebra

Define an unital algebra map
\rho:\mathcal{B}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{B}
such that

\displaystyle\rho\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{j}\left(\eta\right)=\left(c_{j}\curvearrowleft d\right)\left(\eta\right),

for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. The map
\rho
is not coassociative (compare with Theorem
2.8
).

Proposition 5.1
.

For all
i=0,1,2,\ldots,m
and
j=1,2,\ldots,m
;

(i)

\rho\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\right)=a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}
.

(ii)

\rho\circ\theta_{0}=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\rho
.

(iii)

\rho\circ\theta_{k}(a^{j}_{\eta})=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{k}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}
,

for all
j,k=1,2,\ldots,m
and
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
.

Proof:
Let
c\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
, then

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)\emptyset+\sum_{j=0}^{m}x_{j}\left(x_{j}^{-1}\left(c\right)\right).

Thus for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
,

\displaystyle c\curvearrowleft d

\displaystyle=c\left(\mathbf{1}\right)\emptyset+x_{0}\left(x_{0}^{-1}\left(c\right)\curvearrowleft d\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}x_{j}\left(d_{j}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(x_{j}^{-1}\left(c\right)\curvearrowleft d\right)\right).

(i)

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. From (
6
),

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)=a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}\left(c\otimes d\right).

(ii)

Let
a^{j}_{\eta}\in V
. Then,

\displaystyle\left(\rho\circ\theta_{0}\right)(a^{j}_{\eta})\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{j}\left(x_{0}\eta\right)=\left(x_{0}^{-1}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{j}\right)\left(\eta\right).

From (
6
),

\displaystyle\left(\rho\circ\theta_{0}\right)(a^{j}_{\eta})\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(x_{0}^{-1}\left(c_{j}\right)\curvearrowleft d\right)\left(\eta\right)

\displaystyle=\rho\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(x_{0}^{-1}\left(c\right)\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{H}}\right)\circ\rho\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right).

(iii)

For
k=1,2,\ldots,m
;

\displaystyle\left(\rho\circ\theta_{k}\right)\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{j}\right)\left(x_{k}\eta\right)=\left(x_{k}^{-1}\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)_{j}\right)\left(\eta\right).

From (
6
),

\displaystyle\left(\rho\circ\theta_{k}\right)\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(d_{i}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}x_{k}^{-1}\left(c_{j}\right)\curvearrowleft d\right)\left(\eta\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{k}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}\Big)\left(\left(x_{k}^{-1}\left(c\right)\curvearrowleft d\right)\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Big(\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{k}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}\Big)\left(x_{k}^{-1}\left(c\right)\otimes d\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Big(\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{k}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}\Big)\left(c\otimes d\right).

Define the coproduct
\Delta:\mathcal{B}\longrightarrow\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{B}
such that

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a^{j}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c\star d\right)_{j}\left(\eta\right),

for all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
. Since the product,
\star
, is associative (see Theorem
2.7
), the map
\Delta
is coassociative.

Proposition 5.2
.

The coproduct on
V
is defined as

\displaystyle\Delta=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}.

Proof:
Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
and
\eta\in\mbox{$X^{\ast}$}
,

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a^{k}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(c\star d\right)_{k}\left(\eta\right).

Using (
8
),

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a^{k}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(d_{k}{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\left(c_{k}\curvearrowleft d\right)\right)\left(\eta\right)

\displaystyle=\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a^{k}_{\eta}\right)\left(\left(c\curvearrowleft d\right)\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a^{k}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\otimes d\right)

\displaystyle=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a^{k}_{\eta}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right).

Remark
: Observe that
\left(\mathcal{B},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
is not a Hopf algebra as the elements
a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j},j=1,2\ldots,m
are group–like but not invertible.

The coproduct
\Delta
respects the grading of
\mathcal{B}
. The following lemma is necessary to prove this claim. Recall that the length of a word
x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}\cdots x_{i_{k}}
is denoted
\lvert x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}\cdots x_{i_{k}}\rvert(=k)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
n\geq 0
;

\displaystyle\rho\left(V_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}

Proof:
The linear endomorphism
\theta_{i}
is a homogeneous operator of degree
1
on
V
for all
i=0,1,2,\ldots,m
.

\displaystyle|\theta_{i}a_{\eta}^{j}|=\lvert x_{i}\eta\rvert=\lvert\eta\rvert+1.

Therefore,
\theta_{j}:V_{n}\longrightarrow V_{n+1}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
and
n\geq 0
.

The lemma is proven by induction on degree
n
. The base case
(n=0)
is true by Proposition
5.1
.

Assume that the statement holds for all
n\leq k
. Let
a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}\in V_{k+1}
. Then there are two possibilities:

(i)

a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}=a_{x_{0}\eta}^{j}
where
a_{\eta}^{j}\in V_{k}
.

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\rho\circ\theta_{0}\right)\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\rho\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right).\quad(\text{c.f.~Propostion}~\ref{prop:red_coprod_comp})

Note that
\rho\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=k}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}
via induction hypothesis. Hence,

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=k}\theta_{0}\left(V_{i}\right)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=k+1}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}.

(ii)

a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}=a_{x_{p}\eta}^{j}
where
p\neq 0
and
a_{\eta}^{j}\in V_{k}
.

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\rho\circ\theta_{p}\right)\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{p}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Big(\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{p}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}\Big)\quad\quad(\text{c.f.~Proposition}~\ref{prop:red_coprod_comp}).

Recall that
\sum_{(\eta)}a^{j}_{\eta^{\prime}}\otimes a^{p}_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}\in\left(V\otimes V\right)_{k}

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{\tilde{\eta}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\left(\theta_{p}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=k}\rho\left(V_{i}\right)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i_{i}+i_{2}+j=k}\theta_{p}\left(V_{i_{1}}\right)\otimes\hbox{$m$}\left(\mathcal{H}_{i_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=k+1}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}.

Proposition 5.3
.

For all
n\geq 0
;

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\mathcal{B}_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}\mathcal{B}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}

Proof:
Since
\Delta
is an algebra morphism, it is enough to prove that for all
n\geq 0

\displaystyle\Delta\left(V_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\Delta\left(V_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(V_{n}\right)\quad\left(\text{c.f.~Proposition}~\ref{prop:red_coprod_hopf_sec}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}V_{i}\otimes V_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i_{1}+i_{2}+j=n}V_{i_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{i_{2}}\otimes V_{j}\right)\quad\left(\text{c.f.~Lemma}~\ref{lem:grade_preservation}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{j}

The cointeraction between the bialgebras
\left(\mathcal{B},\Delta\right)
and
\left(\mathcal{B},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\right)
is established next.

Theorem 5.3
.

\left(\mathcal{B},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is a right graded comodule bialgebra of
\left(\mathcal{B},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
with the coaction map
\rho
.

Proof:

(i)

\rho
is a coaction follows from its dual statements Theorem
2.4
and Theorem
2.8
.

(ii)

\rho
respects grading of
\mathcal{B}
from Lemma
5.1
.

Remark
:
\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\Delta,\nu,\rho\right)
is a graded connected cointeracting bialgebras with the right coaction map
\rho
. For more details on cointeracting bialgebas and double bialgebras we refer to
[
16
]
.

5.2.
From cointeracting bialgebra
\mathcal{B}
to cointeracting Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}

Observe that
\rho(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-\mathsf{1})=\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-\mathsf{1}\right)\otimes\mathsf{1}
for all
i=1,2,\ldots,m
and the coproduct

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-\mathsf{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\right)-\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}\otimes\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-\mathsf{1}\right)\otimes\mathsf{1}.

Therefore, (as defined earlier in Section
5
) for
\mathfrak{s_{i}}=a_{\mathbf{1}}^{i}-1
and the ideal
\left(\mathfrak{s_{1}},\mathfrak{s_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{s_{m}}\right)
is a bi–ideal of the bialgebra
\left(\mathcal{B},\Delta\right)
.

Defining
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{B}/\left(\mathfrak{s_{1}},\mathfrak{s_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{s_{m}}\right)
provides a connected structure with
\mathcal{H}_{0}\cong{\mathbb{R}}\mathsf{1}
, thus making
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta\right)
a Hopf algebra.

Remark
:  Let
\mathcal{I}:=\left(\mathfrak{s_{1}},\mathfrak{s_{2}},\ldots,\mathfrak{s_{m}}\right)
denote the bi–ideal. Observe that
I
is the bi–ideal for both the cointeracting bialgebras
\left(\mathcal{B},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\right)
and
\left(\mathcal{B},\Delta\right)
. Since,

\displaystyle\rho(\mathfrak{s_{i}})\subseteq\mathfrak{s_{i}}\otimes\mathcal{B}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\rho\left(\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{I}\right)=\rho(\mathcal{H})

\displaystyle\subseteq\rho(\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{I})\otimes\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}\otimes\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{I}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}\right)+\cancel{\left(\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{I}\right)}.

Therefore,
\rho:\mathcal{H}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}
is a right coaction map on Hopf algebra
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\right)
by the Hopf algebra
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta\right)
.

The analogous statements of coproduct
\Delta
and the map
\rho
respects the grading of
\mathcal{H}
follows from Lemma
5.1
and Proposition
5.3
respectively.

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
n\geq 1
;

\displaystyle\rho\left(V_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{j}

Theorem 5.4
.

(1)

For all
n\geq 0
;

\displaystyle\Delta\left(\mathcal{H}_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus\limits_{i+j=n}\mathcal{H}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{j}.

(2)

The graded Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}
is right–handed viz. for all
n\geq 1

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}\left(V_{n}\right)\subseteq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j=n\\
i,j\geq 1\end{subarray}}V_{i}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{j}

where
\Delta^{\prime}(\cdot)=\Delta(\cdot)-\mathsf{1}\otimes\cdot-\cdot\otimes\mathsf{1}
is the reduced coproduct.

Theorem 5.5
.

\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
is a graded connected (right–handed) bialgebra. The character group of
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta,\nu\right)
is isomorphic to the multiplicative feedback group
\left(M^{m},\star\right)
and

\displaystyle\left(M^{m},\star\right)\not\cong\underbrace{\left(M,\star\right)\times\left(M,\star\right)\times\cdots\times\left(M,\star\right)}_{m~\text{times}}\quad\quad\text{for}\;m>1.

The summary of this section so far:

(1)

\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is a graded connected bialgebra.

(2)

The character group of
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is isomorphic to the group
\left(M^{m},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}\right)
.

(3)

\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
is a graded connected right–handed bialgebra.

(4)

The character group of
\left(\mathcal{H},\Delta,\nu\right)
is isomorphic to the group
\left(M^{m},\star\right)
.

The following results stem from Theorem
5.3
.

Theorem 5.6
.

\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}},\nu\right)
is a right graded comodule Hopf algebra of
\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
with the (graded) coaction map
\rho
.

Remark
:  Theorem
5.6
, along with the definition of coproduct
\Delta
as
\Delta=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}
implies that the character group
\left(M^{m},\star\right)
is the Grossman–Larson group of the pre-group
\left(M^{m},{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,},\curvearrowleft\right)
as seen in Theorem
2.10
.

A few examples of the computation of
\rho
on
\mathcal{H}
are (for all
i,j,k=1,2,\ldots,m
):

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{x_{0}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{0}x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{x_{0}x_{0}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{i}\right).

A few examples of the coproduct
\Delta
computation are:

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{0}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{0}x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{0}x_{0}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+2\left(a_{x_{0}}^{i}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{0}x_{0}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{x_{0}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{i}}^{j}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{j}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{i}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{j}\right)+\left(a_{x_{0}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{i}}^{j}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}\otimes\mathsf{1}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{i}\right)+\left(a_{x_{i}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{k}\right)+\left(a_{x_{j}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{i}}^{k}\right)+\left(\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}\right).

Corollary 5.1
.

If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
, then for all
i=1,2,\ldots,m
;

\displaystyle\rho\left(a_{x_{0}^{n}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{x_{0}^{n}}^{i}\otimes\mathsf{1}.

\displaystyle\Delta\left(a_{x_{0}^{n}}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}a_{x_{0}^{k}}^{i}\otimes a_{x_{0}^{n-k}}^{i},

where
a_{x_{0}^{0}}^{i}:=\mathsf{1}
.

Proof:
The proof of is by induction on
n
.

The second identity follows from Proposition
5.2
and (
1
).

The Example
4.10
in
[
19
]
for the computation of feedback group inverse of a one-dimensional series is reworked within our framework.

Example 5.1
.

Let
c=1-x_{1}\,\in{\mathbb{R}}[[x_{1}]]
. Let
S_{\star}
denote the antipode of the graded connected Hopf algebra
\left(\mathcal{H},\hbox{$m$},\mathsf{1},\Delta,\nu\right)
.

\displaystyle\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)(c^{\star-1})=S_{\star}\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)\left(c\right)=-\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)(c)=1.

The reduced coproduct
\Delta^{\prime}\left(\cdot\right):=\Delta\left(\cdot\right)-\mathsf{1}\otimes\cdot-\cdot\otimes\mathsf{1}
. Hence

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)=3a_{x_{1}}^{1}\otimes a_{x_{1}}^{1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)\left(c^{\star-1}\right)

\displaystyle=S_{\star}\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)\left(c\right)=-\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)\left(c\right)-\left(3\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right).S\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)\right)\left(c\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}+3\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)^{2}\right)\left(c\right)=3.

Similarly for computing
a_{x_{1}x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\left(c^{\star-1}\right)
;

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)=\left(4a_{x_{1}}^{1}\otimes a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)+\left(6a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\otimes a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)+3\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\otimes\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)^{2}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)\left(c^{\star-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[-a_{x_{1}x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}-4a_{x_{1}}^{1}.S\left(a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}\right)-6a_{x_{1}x_{1}}^{1}.S\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)-3a_{x_{1}}^{1}.\left(S_{\star}\left(a_{x_{1}}^{1}\right)\right)^{2}\right](c)

\displaystyle=0-4(-1)(3)-6(0)(-1)-3(-1)(1)^{2}=15.

Continuing the computation one obtains
c^{\star-1}=1+x_{1}+3x_{1}^{2}+15x_{1}^{3}+105x_{1}^{4}+\cdots
.

Remark
:  For further part of the document, if
\left(\mathcal{C},\Delta,\epsilon\right)
is a graded connected coalgebra then,
\Delta^{\prime}
denotes the reduced coproduct where

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime}\left(\cdot\right)=\Delta\left(\cdot\right)-\cdot\otimes\mathsf{1}-\mathsf{1}\otimes\cdot

6.
pre-Lie Structure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle

Let
k
be a field of characteristic zero and
V
be a
k
–vector space. The definitions of (right) pre-Lie algebra and pre-Lie coalgebra are recollected briefly. For more details on the pre-Lie algebras, we refer to
[
6
,
27
]
.

Definition 6.1
.

\left(V,\bullet\right)
is a (right) pre-Lie algebra if the magmatic map
\bullet:V^{\otimes 2}\longrightarrow V
satisfies for all
a,b,c\in V
the (right) pre-Lie identity

\displaystyle\left(a\bullet b\right)\bullet c-a\bullet\left(b\bullet c\right)=\left(a\bullet c\right)\bullet b-a\bullet\left(c\bullet b\right).

Define
\left[a,b\right]_{\bullet}:=a\bullet b-b\bullet a
, then
\left(V,\left[\cdot,\cdot\right]_{\bullet}\right)
is a Lie algebra.

Definition 6.2
.

(V,\mathring{\delta})
is a (right) pre-Lie coalgebra if
\mathring{\delta}:V\longrightarrow V^{\otimes 2}
satisfies

\displaystyle({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{V}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{V}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{V}-\tau_{(23)})\circ\Big(\big(\mathring{\delta}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{V}\big)-\big({\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{V}\otimes\mathring{\delta}\big)\Big)\circ\mathring{\delta}=0,

where
\tau_{(23)}:V^{\otimes 3}\to V^{\otimes 3}
,
\tau_{(23)}(a\otimes b\otimes c)=a\otimes c\otimes b
.

Let
\mathcal{S}(V)
be the free symmetric algebra generated by the vector space
V
, with
m
denoting the symmetric product.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{S}(V),\hbox{$m$},\delta,\Delta,\epsilon,\rho)
be graded connected cointeracting bialgebra where

(i)

\left(\mathcal{S}(V),\hbox{$m$},\delta,\epsilon\right)
is a graded connected Hopf algebra in the category of
\left(\mathcal{S}(V),\hbox{$m$},\Delta,\epsilon\right)
right comodule with coaction map
\rho
.

(ii)

\Delta=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\delta
.

(iii)

\delta^{\prime}(V)\subseteq\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{S}^{+}(V)
.

(iv)

For all
x\in V
:
\rho^{\prime}(x):=\rho(x)-x\otimes 1\subseteq V\otimes\mathcal{S}^{+}(V)
.

Then,

(1)

\left(\mathcal{S}(V),\hbox{$m$},\Delta,\epsilon\right)
is a right–handed bialgebra.

(2)

On
V
:
\mathring{\Delta}=\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\delta}
, where

\mathring{\upsilon}:=\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\upsilon
for all
\upsilon\in Hom\left(\mathcal{S}(V),\mathcal{S}(V)\otimes\mathcal{S}(V)\right)
.

where
\pi_{V}
is the canonical projection onto
V
.

Proof:

(1)

It is straightforward to check that bialgebra
\left(\mathcal{S}(V),\hbox{$m$},\Delta\right)
is right–handed.

(2)

On the vector space
V
,

\displaystyle\mathring{\Delta}

\displaystyle=\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\delta

\displaystyle=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\{\left[\rho^{\prime}+\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes 1\right)\right]\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\}\circ\delta

\displaystyle=\{\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{V}\otimes V\right)\circ\left(\rho^{\prime}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\delta\}+\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\delta

\displaystyle=\{\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho^{\prime}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\{1\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}+{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes 1+\delta^{\prime}\}\}+\mathring{\delta}

\displaystyle=\{\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\rho^{\prime}\}+\{\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{1}\right)\circ\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho^{\prime}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\delta^{\prime}\}+\mathring{\delta}

\displaystyle=\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\delta},

since
\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)\circ\rho^{\prime}=\mathring{\rho}=\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)
and
\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho^{\prime}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\delta^{\prime}\in{\rm ker}\left(\pi_{V}\otimes\pi_{V}\right)
.

Remark
:
\mathcal{S}(V)
is a right–hand bialgebra with both coproducts,
\Delta
and
\delta
, thus
V
inherits a graded right pre-Lie coalgebra with
\mathring{\Delta}
and
\mathring{\delta}
, as defined in Proposition
6.1

[
29
]
.

6.1.
pre-Lie Coproduct on
V^{+}

Observe that from Section
5
, the Hopf algebra
\mathcal{H}\cong\mathcal{S}(V^{+})
as
{\mathbb{R}}
-algebras where
V^{+}=\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}V_{n}
.

Corollary 6.1
.

V^{+}
is a graded right pre-Lie coalgebra with the pre-Lie coproducts
\mathring{\Delta}
and
\mathring{\Delta}_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}
with

(15)

\mathring{\Delta}=\mathring{\rho}+\mathring{\Delta}_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}.

Remark
:  It is straightforward to check that for all
a_{\eta}^{i}\in V^{+}

\displaystyle\mathring{\Delta}_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a_{\eta}^{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\pi_{V^{+}}\otimes\pi_{V^{+}}\right)\circ\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}\left(a_{\eta^{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{i}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{i}

where
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}^{\prime}(\eta)=\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta^{\prime\prime}
is the reduced unshuffle coproduct in
{\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle
.

The following proposition computes
\mathring{\rho}
based on the induction on degree of
a_{\eta}^{j}\in V^{+}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

For all
k,j=1,2,\ldots,m
and
i=0,1,\ldots,m

(i)

\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{x_{i}}^{k}\right)=0
.

(ii)

\mathring{\rho}\circ\theta_{0}=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\mathring{\rho}
.

(iii)

\mathring{\rho}\circ\theta_{k}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\big[\mathring{\rho}(a_{\eta}^{j})+\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}\big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}
,

where
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}^{\prime}\left(\eta\right)=\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof:

Since
\rho\left(V^{+}\right)\subseteq V^{+}\otimes\mathcal{H}
(c.f. Lemma
5.2
),

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V^{+}}\right)\circ\rho

(i)

Follows from Proposition
5.1
.

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}\circ\theta_{0}

\displaystyle=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V^{+}}\right)\circ\rho\circ\theta_{0}

\displaystyle=\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V^{+}}\right)\circ\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\rho

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\left({\hbox{$\rm id$}}\otimes\pi_{V^{+}}\right)\circ\rho

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\mathring{\rho}.

(iii)

On
V^{+}\subsetneq\mathcal{B}
,

\displaystyle\rho\circ\theta_{k}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\left(\rho\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{H}}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}+a_{\eta}^{j}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{k}\Big],

where
\Delta_{{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}}^{\prime}\left(\eta\right)=\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}\eta^{\prime}\otimes\eta^{\prime\prime}
is the reduced unshuffle in
{\mathbb{R}}\langle X\rangle
.

Let
\rho\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)=\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta_{(2)}}^{s}+a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{s}
(for some index family
s
). Thus by Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\rho\circ\theta_{k}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}\rho\left(a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\right)\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+\rho\left(a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}\right)\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}+\rho\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes\hbox{$m$}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{s}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\eta^{\prime}_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}_{(2)}}^{s}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}\otimes\mathsf{1}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+a_{\eta_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta_{(2)}}^{s}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{s}a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\eta^{\prime}_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}_{(2)}}^{s}a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}+a_{\eta_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta_{(2)}}^{s}a_{\mathbf{1}}^{k}\Big]

\displaystyle=\Big[\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{B}}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\mathbf{1}}^{s}a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\eta^{\prime}_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}_{(2)}}^{s}a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\eta_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta_{(2)}}^{s}a_{\mathbf{1}}^{k}\Big]\Big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}

On passing to
\mathcal{H}
viz. identify all
a_{\mathbf{1}}^{j}
with the unit
\mathsf{1}
,

\displaystyle\rho\circ\theta_{k}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)=\Big[\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}_{\mathcal{H}}\right)\circ\Big[\sum_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}+a_{\eta_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta_{(2)}}^{s}+\underbrace{a_{\eta^{\prime}_{(1)}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}_{(2)}}^{s}a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}}_{{\rm ker}(\pi_{V^{+}})}\Big]\Big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}\circ\theta_{k}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)=\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\Big[\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)+\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}\Big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}

The following are some computation of
\mathring{\rho}
are (for all
i,j,k,\ell=1,2,\ldots,m
):

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{i}x_{0}}^{j})

\displaystyle=a_{x_{i}}^{j}\otimes a_{x_{0}}^{i}

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{j})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k})

\displaystyle=a_{x_{i}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{i}

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{0}^{2}}^{i})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{0}^{3}}^{i})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{0}x_{i}x_{j}}^{k})

\displaystyle=a_{x_{0}x_{i}}^{k}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{i}

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}(a_{x_{k}x_{i}x_{j}}^{\ell})

\displaystyle=a_{x_{k}}^{\ell}\otimes a_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{k}+2a_{x_{k}x_{i}}^{\ell}\otimes a_{x_{j}}^{k}+a_{x_{k}x_{j}}^{\ell}\otimes a_{x_{i}}^{k}.

Remark
:  For all
n\geq 1
and
j=1,2,\ldots,m
:
a_{x_{0}^{n}}^{j}\in{\rm ker}\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)
.

Graded dual of
\mathring{\rho}

The graded dual of
V^{+}
is identified with proper polynomials
\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}\subsetneq\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}\langle\langle X\rangle\rangle$}
; with dual basis
\eta e_{j}
where
\eta\in X^{+}
and
e_{j}
for
j=1,2,\ldots,m
are standard unit vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
such that
a_{\eta}^{j}\left(\zeta e_{k}\right)=\delta_{\eta,\zeta}\delta_{j,k}
for all
\zeta\in X^{+}
.

The vector space
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle
is equipped with a magmatic product
\triangleleft:\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}^{\otimes 2}\longrightarrow\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}
such that for
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}

\displaystyle(c\triangleleft d)_{i}\left(\eta\right)=a_{\eta}^{i}\left(c\triangleleft d\right)=\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{i}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right),

for all
a_{\eta}^{i}\in V^{+}
. Dualizing Theorem
6.1
, the following theorem is obtained:

Theorem 6.2
.

For all
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}
and
j=1,2,\ldots,m
.

(i)

x_{0}e_{j}\triangleleft d=0

(ii)

x_{k}e_{j}\triangleleft d=x_{k}d_{k}e_{j}\quad\quad\forall\,k=1,2,\ldots,m
.

(iii)

x_{0}c\triangleleft d=x_{0}\left(c\triangleleft d\right)
.

(iv)

x_{k}c\triangleleft d=x_{k}\left(c\triangleleft d\right)+x_{k}\left(c{\,{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 3.5pt\vrule height=5.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}\hrule}\vbox{\hbox{\vrule\hskip 2.6pt\vrule height=3.0pt,width=0.0pt\vrule}\hrule}}}}}\,}_{k}\,d\right)\quad\quad\forall\,k=1,2,\ldots,m
.

Proof:
For
d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}
and
\eta\in X^{+},k\neq 0

\displaystyle a_{x_{0}\eta}^{j}(x_{0}e_{j}\triangleleft d)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)\left(x_{0}e_{j}\otimes d\right)=0

\displaystyle a_{x_{k}\eta}^{j}\left(x_{0}e_{j}\triangleleft d\right)

\displaystyle=\Big[\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\Big[\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)+\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}\Big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}\Big](x_{0}e_{j}\otimes d)=0.

Similarly,

\displaystyle a_{x_{k}\eta}^{j}\left(x_{k}e_{j}\triangleleft d\right)

\displaystyle=\Big[\left(\theta_{k}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\Big[\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)+\sum\limits_{\left(\eta\right)}a_{\eta^{\prime}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta^{\prime\prime}}^{k}\Big]+a_{x_{k}}^{j}\otimes a_{\eta}^{k}\Big](x_{k}e_{j}\otimes d)

\displaystyle\quad=a_{\eta}^{k}\left(d\right)=a_{x_{k}\eta}^{j}\left(x_{k}d_{k}e_{j}\right)

\displaystyle a_{x_{0}\eta}^{j}\left(x_{k}e_{j}\triangleleft d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{j}\right)\left(x_{k}e_{j}\otimes d\right)=0

\displaystyle\quad=a_{x_{0}\eta}^{j}(x_{k}d_{k}e_{j}).

Let
c,d\in\mbox{${\mathbb{R}}^{m}_{p}\langle X\rangle$}
and
\eta\in X^{+}
. Using Theorem
6.1
,

\displaystyle a_{x_{0}\eta}^{i}\left(c\triangleleft d\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{0}\otimes{\hbox{$\rm id$}}\right)\circ\mathring{\rho}\left(a_{\eta}^{i}\right)\left(c\otimes d\right)
